# Pinned macOS Keymap Layer Requirements

**Status:** Static layer and binding inventory complete; semantics, macOS dispatch, and end-to-end acceptance remain open
**Baseline:** Zed commit `3569541038dd51524b03998ba4d38d253cb54f80`
**First acceptance platform:** macOS
**Owner:** Keymap, with each action's canonical capability owner

This independently written register enumerates the macOS base-profile overlays, the conditional Vim/Helix layer, and the macOS-specific override layer. It complements the [default platform action register](action-requirements.md), which contains the three default platform maps. Each binding row preserves its key sequence, context, key-equivalent flag, action identifier, arguments, and explicit null entries. Rows link to a stable action ID where one exists.

Static binding presence is not proof that the selected profile loads, a chord dispatches, the context is active, or the action succeeds. The Linux runtime dump verifies Linux action registration and declared aliases only; it does not verify macOS registration or behavior.

## Layer composition

The pinned behavior composes the platform default map with the selected base-profile overlay, conditionally adds the Vim/Helix layer when either mode is enabled, then applies platform-specific overrides and user bindings. The `None` profile disables the built-in layers while user bindings remain separate. Verify precedence, shadowing, explicit null behavior, profile changes, and reload behavior in the runtime. Labonair's canonical Keymap editor is opened from the titlebar global menu, Settings, or Command Palette. Its distinct management, editable JSON, and bundled-default entry routes are classified in the [Keymap entry action packet](action-classification-keymap-entry.md). Keymap owns binding state and runtime composition; the profile and mode value/persistence boundary remains an architecture requirement to resolve.

The default macOS map contains 930 active binding entries, two explicit null entries, and 585 action identifiers. The macOS-specific override layer adds 12 distinct identifiers absent from all three default platform maps, so the default macOS map plus that override layer contains 597 distinct action identifiers before any selected profile, Vim/Helix mode, or user overrides.

|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `<code>KMP-MACOS-DEFAULT</code>` | Platform default map | 133 | 932 total: 930 active + 2 null | 585 | 2 | 0 |
| `<code>KMP-MACOS-NONE</code>` | No built-in map (profile setting) | — | — | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-ATOM</code> | Atom base-profile overlay | 8 | 57 | 44 | 0 | 5 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-CURSOR</code> | Cursor base-profile overlay | 8 | 29 | 17 | 0 | 0 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-EMACS</code> | Emacs base-profile overlay | 10 | 123 | 76 | 6 | 7 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-JETBRAINS</code> | JetBrains base-profile overlay | 22 | 143 | 102 | 8 | 6 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-SUBLIME-TEXT</code> | Sublime Text base-profile overlay | 6 | 67 | 40 | 0 | 7 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-TEXTMATE</code> | TextMate base-profile overlay | 8 | 45 | 42 | 0 | 4 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-BASE-VSCODE</code> | VS Code base-profile overlay | 10 | 23 | 19 | 3 | 2 |
| <code>KMP-MODE-VIM-HELIX</code> | Conditional Vim/Helix mode layer | 70 | 894 | 437 | 8 | 285 |
| <code>KMP-MACOS-PLATFORM-OVERRIDES</code> | macOS-specific overrides | 4 | 21 | 16 | 0 | 12 |
| `<code>KMP-USER-OVERRIDES</code>` | User keymap file (dynamic, not statically inventoried here) | — | — | — | — | — |

The nine enumerated overlay assets contain 1,402 binding entries: 1,377 action bindings and 25 explicit null bindings. Their union contains 578 raw action identifiers. Of these, 265 also occur in at least one default platform map; 312 more are registered by the pinned Linux runtime but absent from all three default maps. Two spellings resolve through declared deprecated aliases: `agent::OpenRulesLibrary` → `agent::ManageSkills` and `project_panel::CopyPath` → `workspace::CopyPath`. These Linux registrations and aliases still require direct macOS verification.

The macOS-specific layer contains 21 entries, 16 distinct action identifiers, and 12 identifiers not found in any of the three platform default files. Those 12 are all registered in the pinned Linux runtime; the default macOS runtime still needs direct capture and dispatch verification.

## Profile and layer requirements

| Requirement ID | User goal and acceptance flow | Conditions and settings | Owner / entry point | Acceptance status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KMP-MACOS-DEFAULT` | Load the macOS default bindings and preserve every key, context, argument, and explicit null row. Verify dispatch and an unaffected context for each binding. | macOS platform; default base profile; no mode overlay unless enabled; user overrides may supersede defaults. | Keymap / titlebar global menu → Keymap; Settings → Keymap; Command Palette. | Static map recorded; runtime dispatch and action effects pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-NONE` | Select None and verify built-in bindings, mode bindings, and macOS overrides are disabled while the user's own keymap remains active. | Base Keymap = None; verify the value can be set, saved, reloaded, and reset. The pinned typed enum contains None although the macOS selector option list omits it. | Keymap; profile value in Settings or user settings file. | Static setting state recorded; UI reachability and effective behavior pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-ATOM` | Select Atom bindings, use an overlaid chord, then return to the default profile and verify the original binding. | Base Keymap = Atom; verify default inheritance, collision resolution, profile persistence, and reload. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-CURSOR` | Select Cursor bindings and verify each overlay chord in its declared context. | Base Keymap = Cursor; include default inheritance and user override precedence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-EMACS` | Select Emacs bindings and verify chords, explicit unbindings, kill-ring behavior, and mode/context exclusions. | Base Keymap = Emacs; verify explicit null rows and profile persistence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-JETBRAINS` | Select JetBrains bindings and verify overlay chords and explicit unbindings. | Base Keymap = JetBrains; verify inherited conflicts, null behavior, and profile persistence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-SUBLIME-TEXT` | Select Sublime Text bindings and verify overlay chords in the declared contexts. | Base Keymap = Sublime Text; verify default inheritance and user override precedence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-TEXTMATE` | Select TextMate bindings and verify overlay chords in the declared contexts. | Base Keymap = TextMate; verify default inheritance and user override precedence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-BASE-VSCODE` | Select VS Code bindings and verify overlay chords, explicit unbindings, and context exclusions. | Base Keymap = VS Code; verify default inheritance and profile persistence. | Keymap; profile value in Settings. | Static overlay recorded; profile selection and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MODE-VIM-HELIX` | Enable Vim or Helix mode and verify modal bindings by mode, including movement, operators, selections, insert/normal transitions, and explicit unbindings; disable the mode and verify the base profile returns. | `vim_mode` or `helix_mode`; determine precedence when both are enabled and how mode values persist. | Keymap; Settings values and Keymap editor. | Static layer recorded; conditional loading, mode conflict, behavior, and runtime acceptance pending. |
| `KMP-MACOS-PLATFORM-OVERRIDES` | Preserve macOS-specific shortcut overrides after profile and mode layers; verify each changed chord and context. | macOS platform, selected base profile, optional Vim/Helix layer; then apply user bindings. | Keymap; global app input. | Static layer recorded; effective dispatch and shadowing pending. |
| `KMP-USER-OVERRIDES` | Add, edit, remove, reset, save, reload, and recover a user binding while preserving profile defaults and reporting conflicts. | User keymap file, invalid JSONC, duplicate chords, context conflicts, explicit null, reset, external file changes. | Keymap editor in the Workspace Keymap tab. | Dynamic user file behavior remains open. |

The profile and mode flows correspond to the existing [Keymap Settings requirements](settings-requirements.md) and the [Keymap surface crosswalk](settings-crosswalk.md). They do not move binding persistence or the binding editor into Settings.

## Current Labonair counterpart and gap

- Labonair already has the Keymap owner and its canonical Workspace Keymap tab, reachable from the titlebar menu, Settings link, and Command Palette.
- The local Keymap package currently has the default macOS and Linux maps but no selected base-profile overlay loader or profile setting. The pinned `base_keymap` requirement therefore has no local counterpart yet; its owner, storage, selector, scope, default, and reload contract must be settled before implementation.
- Labonair has an Editor-owned `editor.vimMode` value and modal editing behavior. This is a partial behavioral counterpart to the pinned `vim_mode` flow, with a different owner/path; no local Helix mode or combined Vim/Helix binding layer was found.
- The pinned macOS profile assets and their static rows above do not establish Labonair action ownership or routes. Every action remains pending end-to-end classification and runtime verification.

## Binding requirements

Each row is a stable binding requirement. An empty key-equivalent field in the asset is preserved as `not set`; its effective interpretation must be checked in the runtime. Explicit null rows require evidence that the inherited binding is suppressed in this exact layer and context.

Binding IDs are deterministic for this pinned inventory: they incorporate the layer, source-block order, context, key sequence, action, and arguments. The default-map block IDs separately identify the platform, block order, context, and key-equivalent flag. Rebuild the requirement set when the reference commit changes.

## Overlay alias reconciliation

| Alias identifier | Linux declared target | Canonical action requirement ID | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `agent::OpenRulesLibrary` | `agent::ManageSkills` | `ACT-REGISTRY-LINUX-agent-ManageSkills` | Alias metadata is present in the pinned Linux runtime; verify macOS registration, binding dispatch, label, and visible outcome. |
| `project_panel::CopyPath` | `workspace::CopyPath` | `ACT-PLATFORM-project_panel-CopyPath` | Alias metadata is present in the pinned Linux runtime; verify macOS registration and effective copy behavior. |

## Remaining acceptance

- Test every selected base profile, Vim/Helix enablement, profile/mode transition, and user override against the pinned macOS runtime; record platform, window size, theme, scale, and active/inactive context.
- Classify each linked action's user goal, effect, state changes, arguments, settings, persistence, failure/cancellation/recovery behavior, owner, typed route, and dependencies. Binding rows do not inherit semantics from their names.
- Confirm both declared aliases on macOS and check the effective precedence of platform and user layers.
- Inventory the Linux alternate-profile overlays and remaining platform maps, then reconcile them with the same action IDs and per-binding requirements.
- The analysis and implementation are in the same session. This register is independently written from the pinned behavior inventory; it does not claim personnel- or session-separated clean-room provenance.
- Native paired macOS runtime and visual capture is unavailable on this host; this gate blocks R07-000 and the overall goal until evidence is produced.
